Description
  Text
  Posaconazole is an azole antifungal agent indicated for the treatment of invasive aspergillosis and oropharyngeal candidiasis, including oropharyngeal candidiasis refractory to itraconazole and/or fluconazole, and for prophylaxis of invasive Aspergillus and Candida infections in severely immunocompromised patients, such as hematopoietic stem cell transplant recipients with graft-versus-host disease or those with hematologic malignancies with prolonged neutropenia from chemotherapy. It is also used as an alternative therapy for other invasive fungal infections such as cryptococcosis, coccidioidomycosis, and histoplasmosis. Posaconazole oral dose formulations are not interchangeable due to differences in the dosing of each formulation. Posaconazole has been shown to prolong the QTc interval and cause torsade de pointes (TdP); elevations in liver function tests may also occur with posaconazole therapy. Posaconazole was FDA-approved in 2006.

Mechanism Of Action
  Text
  Posaconazole exerts its effect by altering the fungal cell membrane. Azole antifungals alter the fungal cell membrane by inhibiting ergosterol synthesis through an interaction with 14-alpha demethylase, a cytochrome P-450 enzyme needed to convert lanosterol to ergosterol, an essential component of the membrane. Inhibition of ergosterol synthesis results in increased cellular permeability causing leakage of cellular contents. In contrast, amphotericin B binds to ergosterol after it is synthesized. Posaconazole is significantly more potent at inhibiting 14-alpha demethylase than itraconazole. Azole compounds have a broad spectrum of antifungal activity against common fungal pathogens.
 
Cross-resistance of posaconazole with other azole antifungals may be possible due to common modes of action. Clinical isolates of Candida albicans and Candida glabrata with decreased posaconazole susceptibility were observed in oral swish samples taken during prophylaxis studies with posaconazole and fluconazole. This suggests a potential for development of resistance. These isolates also had reduced susceptibility to other azoles. Due to the potential for cross-resistance, specific organism susceptibility data should be reviewed before selecting an antifungal for the treatment of infections.

Pharmacokinetics
  Route Specific Pharmacokinetics
    Name
    Oral Route
    Description
    Delayed-release oral suspension: The absolute bioavailability of the delayed-release oral suspension is approximately 70% to 80%. The effect of food on the pharmacokinetics of the delayed-release oral suspension has not been determined.
Delayed-release tablets: After oral administration in healthy adults, posaconazole delayed-release tablets are absorbed with a median Tmax of 4 to 5 hours. The absolute bioavailability is approximately 54%. The mean elimination half-life is 26 to 31 hours, and steady-state plasma concentrations are reached within 6 days. Posaconazole exhibits dose-proportional pharmacokinetics after single and multiple doses up to 300 mg. Administration with a high-fat meal increases Cmax by 16% and AUC by 51% compared with the fasted state.
Immediate-release oral suspension: After oral administration, posaconazole immediate-release oral suspension is absorbed with a median Tmax of approximately 3 to 5 hours. Dose-proportional increases in AUC occur after single doses of 50 to 800 mg and after multiple doses of 50 to 400 mg twice daily; no further increases in AUC are observed with doses above 400 mg twice daily in febrile neutropenic individuals or those with refractory invasive fungal infections. Steady-state plasma concentrations are achieved within 7 to 10 days. The mean elimination half-life is approximately 35 hours (range: 20 to 66 hours). Food significantly increases absorption of the immediate-release suspension. Relative to the fasted state, after a single 200-mg dose, mean AUC and Cmax are increased approximately 3-fold with a nonfat meal and 4-fold with a high-fat meal (approximately 50 g fat). After a single 400-mg dose, mean AUC and Cmax are increased approximately 3-fold when administered with a liquid nutritional supplement (14 g fat). Gastric acid conditions may also affect absorption. In a single-dose, crossover study in healthy adults, coadministration with an acidic beverage (ginger ale) increased Cmax by 92% and AUC by 70%, whereas coadministration with esomeprazole decreased Cmax by 46% and AUC by 32% compared with posaconazole alone. Coadministration with both ginger ale and esomeprazole resulted in decreases in Cmax by 33% and AUC by 21% relative to posaconazole alone.
    Name
    Intravenous Route
    Description
    After intravenous administration in healthy adults, posaconazole exhibits dose-proportional pharmacokinetics. The mean elimination half-life is approximately 27 hours, and total body clearance is 7.3 L/hour.
  Special Populations
    Name
    Hepatic Impairment
    Description
    Hepatic dysfunction alters the pharmacokinetic parameters of posaconazole. After a single 400 mg dose of posaconazole immediate-release oral suspension, the mean AUC was 43%, 27%, and 21% higher in subjects with mild (Child-Pugh Class A, n = 6), moderate (Child-Pugh Class B, n = 6), or severe (Child-Pugh Class C, n = 6) hepatic impairment, respectively, compared to subjects with normal hepatic function (n = 18). Compared to subjects with normal hepatic function, the mean Cmax was 1% higher, 40% higher, and 34% lower in subjects with mild, moderate, or severe hepatic impairment, respectively. No dedicated pharmacokinetic studies have been conducted in individuals with hepatic impairment using the delayed-release tablet, delayed-release oral suspension, or intravenous formulation.
    Name
    Renal Impairment
    Description
    After oral administration of posaconazole, no clinically significant differences in pharmacokinetics were observed in individuals with an eGFR of 20 mL/minute/1.73 m2 or more compared with those with an eGFR more than 80 mL/minute/1.73 m2. Although the mean posaconazole plasma exposure (AUC) was similar in individuals with an eGFR less than 20 mL/minute/1.73 m2 receiving posaconazole oral suspension and those with an eGFR greater than 80 mL/minute/1.73 m2, exposure variability was substantially higher in the severe renal impairment group, with a coefficient of variation of 96% compared with less than 40% in individuals with normal renal function. Similar posaconazole pharmacokinetic findings are expected after administration of posaconazole delayed-release tablets. Accumulation of the intravenous vehicle, SBECD, is expected in individuals with moderate to severe renal impairment (eGFR less than 50 mL/minute/1.73 m2) who are receiving posaconazole injection. Posaconazole is not removed by hemodialysis.
    Name
    Pediatrics
    Description
    Based on a population pharmacokinetic model evaluating posaconazole pharmacokinetics and predicting exposures in pediatric patients, mean steady-state average posaconazole concentrations (Cavg) greater than or equal to 700 ng/mL are achieved in approximately 90% of patients with the recommended dose of posaconazole injection and posaconazole delayed-release oral suspension. The population pharmacokinetic analysis of posaconazole in pediatric patients suggests that age, sex, renal impairment, and ethnicity have no clinically meaningful effect on the pharmacokinetics of posaconazole.
Delayed-release oral suspension: In a multiple-dose pharmacokinetic study in pediatric patients receiving posaconazole delayed-release oral suspension at a dose of 6 mg/kg/dose (Max: 300 mg/dose) once daily, with twice-daily dosing on day 1, mean posaconazole Cmax, median Tmax, and mean clearance were 1,510 ng/mL, 4 hours, and 4.6 L/hour, respectively, in patients 2 to 6 years (n = 7) and 1,370 ng/mL, 2.78 hours, and 8.39 L/hour, respectively, in patients 7 to 17 years (n = 12). In pediatric patients treated for invasive aspergillosis, steady-state pharmacokinetic evaluations demonstrated a mean Cmax of approximately 2,220 ng/mL after delayed-release oral suspension in patients 2 to 11 years.
Delayed-release tablets: In pediatric patients 12 to 17 years treated for invasive aspergillosis and receiving posaconazole delayed-release tablets, steady-state pharmacokinetic evaluations demonstrated a mean Cmax of approximately 2,250 ng/mL, with a median Tmax of approximately 7.15 hours.
Immediate-release oral suspension: Based on data from 10 pediatric patients (13 to 17 years) receiving an oral suspension dose of 200 mg 3 times daily, the mean steady-state average posaconazole concentration (Cavg) was similar to the concentration observed in adults. However, in another study in neutropenic pediatric patients (n = 136; 11 months to 18 years), the target posaconazole Cavg of 500 to 2,500 ng/mL was attained by only 50% of patients instead of the study's pre-specified goal of 90%.
Intravenous: In a multiple-dose pharmacokinetic study in pediatric patients who received posaconazole injection at a dose of 6 mg/kg/dose (Max: 300 mg/dose) once daily, with twice-daily dosing on day 1, mean posaconazole Cmax, median Tmax, and mean clearance were 3,060 ng/mL, 1.75 hours, and 3.27 L/hour, respectively, in patients 2 to 6 years (n = 17) and 3,340 ng/mL, 1.77 hours, and 4.76 L/hour, respectively, in patients 7 to 17 years (n = 24). In pediatric patients treated for invasive aspergillosis, mean steady-state Cmax values after intravenous administration were approximately 3,630 ng/mL in patients 2 to 11 years and approximately 3,510 ng/mL in patients 12 to 17 years.
    Name
    Geriatric
    Description
    The pharmacokinetics of posaconazole are not significantly affected by age.
    Name
    Gender Differences
    Description
    The pharmacokinetics of posaconazole are not significantly affected by sex.
    Name
    Ethnic Differences
    Description
    In a population pharmacokinetic analysis, posaconazole exposure (AUC) was 25% higher in Chinese patients relative to patients from other races or ethnicities. This higher exposure is not expected to be clinically relevant.
    Name
    Obesity
    Description
    Body weight has a clinically significant effect on posaconazole clearance. Relative to a 70-kg person, the Cavg is decreased by 25% in patients weighing more than 120 kg. Patients weighting more than 120 kg may be at higher risk for lower posaconazole plasma concentrations compared to lower weight patients.
  Text
  Posaconazole is administered orally and intravenously. Posaconazole has significant penetration into the body tissues, with the mean volume of distribution being 261 L (range: 226 to 295 L). Protein binding is more than 98%, predominantly to albumin. In plasma, posaconazole mainly circulates as the parent compound. The major circulating metabolites are glucuronide conjugates formed via UDP glucuronidation (phase 2 enzymes). There are no major circulating CYP450 (oxidative) metabolites; however, posaconazole is reported to inhibit CYP3A4 enzymes. Based on radiolabeled studies, it is mainly eliminated in the feces (71% of the radiolabeled dose up to 120 hours) primarily as parent drug (66% of radiolabeled dose). Urinary and fecal excretion account for approximately 17% of the excreted metabolites. Renal elimination is a minor pathway, with 13% of the radiolabeled dose excreted in urine up to 120 hours (less than 0.2% of the radiolabeled dose is parent drug).
 
Affected cytochrome P450 isoenzymes and drug transporters: CYP3A4, P-gp
Posaconazole is metabolized via UDP glucuronidation and is a substrate for and may inhibit P-glycoprotein (P-gp). It is a potent inhibitor of CYP3A4.

Administration
  General Information
  For storage information, see the specific product information within the How Supplied section.
  Route Specific Administrations
    Administration Details
      Name
      Oral Solid Formulations
      Description
      Swallow tablets whole; do not divide, chew, or crush.
Administer with or without food.
For patients who cannot eat a full meal and who can swallow tablets whole, use the delayed-released tablets instead of the immediate-release oral suspension for indications other than oropharyngeal candidiasis. The delayed-release tablets generally provide higher plasma drug exposures than the immediate-release oral suspension under both fed and fasted conditions.
      Name
      Oral Liquid Formulations
      Description
      Immediate-release oral suspension
Shake well before each use.
Administer using a calibrated measuring device. A measured dosing spoon is provided, marked for doses of 2.5 mL and 5 mL.
Administer during or immediately after (i.e., within 20 minutes) a full meal.
For patients who cannot eat a full meal and who can swallow tablets whole, use the delayed-release tablets instead of oral suspension for indications other than oropharyngeal candidiasis.
For patients who cannot eat a full meal and for whom the delayed-release tablets or injection are not options, administer each dose of oral suspension with a liquid nutritional supplement or an acidic carbonated beverage (e.g., ginger ale).
For patients who cannot eat a full meal or tolerate an oral nutritional supplement or an acidic carbonated beverage and who do not have the option of taking the delayed-release tablets or injection, consider an alternative antifungal therapy or monitor patients closely for breakthrough fungal infections if continuing to use the oral suspension.
Rinse the measuring device with water after each administration and before storage.
 
PowderMix for delayed-release oral suspension
To ensure delivery of the correct dose, only the provided notched tip syringes MUST be used for preparation and administration. The design of the notched tip syringe prevents aggregation of the suspension during preparation and administration. Do not administer with ENFit oral syringes or use the notched syringes with ENFit connectors in patients with enteral feeding tubes.
 
Preparation
Do not open packet in the PowderMix kit until ready to prepare the medicine.
Only use the mixing liquid in the kit to prepare the PowderMix for delayed-release oral suspension.
Remove cap from the mixing liquid and push the bottle adapter into the neck of the bottle. Once in place, the bottle adapter stays in the bottle.
Remove 9 mL of mixing liquid using the provided BLUE syringe. Put the cap back on the bottle.
Using the provided mixing cup, combine 9 mL of mixing liquid and the entire contents of 1 packet in the PowderMix kit and mix. Each single-use packet in the PowderMix kit contains 300 mg of posaconazole to be suspended in 9 mL of mixing liquid giving a final concentration of approximately 30 mg/mL.
Shake the mixing cup vigorously for 45 seconds to mix the powder and mixing liquid from the PowderMix kit. Check to make sure the powder is mixed. The mixture should look cloudy and free of clumps.
The reconstituted suspension must be used within 1 hour. Discard unused portion of the prepared drug product.
 
Administration
Administer with food.
Administration with alcohol is not recommended.
Choose the correct syringe based on the prescribed dose:
Use 3 mL (GREEN) syringe if dose is 3 mL or less.
Use 10 mL (BLUE) syringe if dose is more than 3 mL.
Measure the prescribed dose volume with the notched tip syringe provided with the kit and administer the dose orally. Administer the dose orally within 1 hour of mixing.
Not all the PowderMix in the mixing cup will be used. There will be some left over in the mixing cup.
The maximum dose that can be accurately withdrawn from the mixing cup after reconstitution is 240 mg (8 mL).
Discard any remaining suspension. The mixing cup may be hand washed and reused. Alternatively, the mixing cup may be discarded, and a similar mixing cup with a lid may be used for subsequent doses.
The notched tip syringes may also be hand washed and reused. For additional supply, a separate box of notched tip syringes is provided with the PowderMix kit.
    Name
    Oral Administration
    Description
    The delayed-release tablets, delayed-release oral suspension, and immediate-release oral suspension are not interchangeable due to differences in dosing. These formulations are supplied in different dose strengths of posaconazole, are approved for different indications, age groups, and weights, and have different dosages, durations of therapy, and preparation and administration instructions. Select the appropriate dosage form based on the indication, age group, and weight, and carefully follow the recommended dosage, preparation, and administration instructions for each product.
    Administration Details
      Name
      Intravenous Administration
      Description
      Dilution
Bring refrigerated vial to room temperature before dilution.
Adults: Aseptically transfer 1 vial (16.7 mL) of posaconazole injection (containing 300 mg of posaconazole) to an intravenous bag or bottle containing a compatible diluent to achieve a final concentration of 1 to 2 mg/mL.
Pediatrics: Aseptically transfer the appropriate dose of posaconazole injection (18 mg/mL) to an intravenous bag or bottle containing a compatible diluent to achieve a final concentration of 1 to 2 mg/mL. Discard any unused portion from the vial.
Compatible diluents include 5% Dextrose Injection, 0.45% Sodium Chloride Injection, 0.9% Sodium Chloride Injection, 5% Dextrose and 0.45% Sodium Chloride Injection, 5% Dextrose and 0.9% Sodium Chloride Injection, 5% Dextrose Injection with 20 mEq potassium chloride. Avoid use of other diluents as this may result in particulate formation.
Administer immediately after dilution, as the solution does not contain preservatives.
Storage: Diluted solution may be stored for up to 24 hours under refrigeration at 2 to 8 degrees C (36 to 46 degrees F); discard any unused portion.
 
Intermittent IV Infusion
Use of a 0.22-micron polyethersulfone (PES) or polyvinylidene difluoride (PVDF) filter is required.
Administer by slow IV infusion over approximately 90 minutes through a central line. Do not administer as an IV bolus injection.
When a central line is temporarily not available, a single dose of the diluted solution may be infused through a peripheral venous catheter over approximately 30 minutes. Do not administer multiple peripheral infusions as this may result in infusion site reactions.
    Name
    Injectable Administration
    Description
    Visually inspect parenteral products for particulate matter and discoloration prior to administration whenever solution and container permit. The diluted solution ranges from colorless to yellow.

Maximum Dosage Limits
  Age Groups
    Name
    Adults
    Max Dosage
    delayed-release tablet, loading dose of 600 mg/day and maintenance dose of 300 mg/day PO; immediate-release suspension, 800 mg/day PO; intravenous, loading dose of 600 mg/day and maintenance dose of 300 mg/day IV. Safety and efficacy of delayed-release suspension have not been established.

    Geriatric

    Name
    Adolescents
    Max Dosage
    weighing more than 40 kg: delayed-release tablet, loading dose of 600 mg/day and maintenance dose of 300 mg/day PO; immediate-release suspension, 800 mg/day PO; intravenous, loading dose of 12 mg/kg/day (Max: 600 mg/day) and maintenance dose of 6 mg/kg/day (Max: 300 mg/day) IV. Safety and efficacy of delayed-release suspension have not been established.
weighing 36 to 40 kg: delayed-release suspension, loading dose of 480 mg/day and maintenance dose of 240 mg/day PO; immediate-release suspension, 800 mg/day PO; intravenous, loading dose of 12 mg/kg/day and maintenance dose of 6 mg/kg/day IV. Safety and efficacy of delayed-release tablet have not been established.
weighing 26 to 35 kg: delayed-release suspension, loading dose of 420 mg/day and maintenance dose of 210 mg/day PO; immediate-release suspension, 800 mg/day PO; intravenous, loading dose of 12 mg/kg/day and maintenance dose of 6 mg/kg/day IV. Safety and efficacy of delayed-release tablet have not been established.
weighing 21 to 25 kg: delayed-release suspension, loading dose of 360 mg/day and maintenance dose of 180 mg/day PO; immediate-release  suspension, 800 mg/day PO; intravenous, loading dose of 12 mg/kg/day and maintenance dose of 6 mg/kg/day IV. Safety and efficacy of delayed-release tablet have not been established.
    Name
    Children
    Max Dosage
    2 to 12 years weighing more than 40 kg: delayed-release tablet, loading dose of 600 mg/day and maintenance dose of 300 mg/day PO; intravenous, loading dose of 12 mg/kg/day (Max: 600 mg/day) and maintenance dose of 6 mg/kg/day (Max: 300 mg/day) IV. Safety and efficacy of immediate-release suspension have not been established; however, doses up to 24 mg/kg/day or 1,200 mg/day PO have been used off-label. Safety and efficacy of delayed-release suspension have not been established.

Safety and efficacy of delayed-release tablets have not been established.

Dosage Adjustment Guidelines
  Hepatic Impairment
  Child-Pugh Class A, B, or C: No dosage adjustment is needed.
  Renal Impairment
  Oral formulations
CrCl 20 mL/minute/1.73 m2 or more: No dosage adjustment is needed.
CrCl less than 20 mL/minute/1.73 m2: Due to a variability in exposure, closely monitor individuals with severe renal impairment for breakthrough invasive fungal infections.
 
Injectable formulation
CrCl 50 mL/minute/1.73 m2 or more: No dosage adjustment is needed.
CrCl less than 50 mL/minute/1.73 m2: Avoid use unless the expected benefit justifies the potential risk of accumulation of the IV vehicle, betadex sulfobutyl ether sodium (SBECD). If use is necessary, closely monitor serum creatinine and consider switching to oral therapy if renal function worsens.
